s^{\prime}\!=\!s^{\tt v}
)

To achieve factorization in
dCBN
and
dCBV
via the abstract
method, we need to establish the existence of an
SFS
in each case.
This requires validating multiple commutations. We bypass these
lengthy proofs by adopting a simpler projection approach
from
dBANG
.

As in
dBANG
, we claim that surface reduction is the significant
part of full reduction in
dCBN
/
dCBV
, and we consequently
identify the irrelevant subreduction, called here

internal
. The
dCBN
(resp.
dCBV
) internal contexts

{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
(resp.
{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}
) are
full contexts whose hole is in an argument (resp. under a
\lambda
).
Formally,

(
dCBN
Internal Contexts)

\displaystyle{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}

\displaystyle\;\Coloneqq\;t\,{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\;|\;t[x\backslash{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}]\;|\;{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{*}\!\left<{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\right>

\displaystyle\text{with }{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{*}\in\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\setminus\{\diamond\}

(
dCBV
Internal Contexts)

\displaystyle{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}

\displaystyle\;\Coloneqq\;\lambda x.{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}\;|\;{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{*}\!\left<{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}\right>

\displaystyle\text{with }{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{*}\in\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}\setminus\{\diamond\}

The
dCBN
(resp.
dCBV
) internal reduction

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}
(resp.
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}}
) is the closure over all
internal contexts
{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
(resp.
{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}
) of the rewrite rules
dB
and
s
(resp.
dB
and
sV
). For example,

(\lambda x.x)\diamond
is a
dCBN
internal context, while

\diamond
is not, thus
(\lambda x.x)((\lambda y.z)t)\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}(\lambda x.x)z\not\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}z
. And

(\lambda x.\diamond)z
is a
dCBV
internal context while

\diamond
is not, thus
(\lambda x.(\lambda y.y)z)z\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}}(\lambda x.y[y\backslash z])z\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}}(\lambda x.z)z\not\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}}z[x\backslash z]
.

As in the surface case, the one-step simulation and reverse simulation
(
Lems.

2
and
3
for
dCBN
,

Lems.

5
and
6

for
dCBV
) can be specialized to the internal case. This allows us
to show in particular the following property.

Corollary 8

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
and
s^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
.

•

(Stability)
: if
t^{\tt n}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}}s^{\prime}
(resp.
t^{\tt v}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}}s^{\prime}
and

s^{\prime}
is a
\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF
) then there is
s\in\Lambda

such that
s^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}
(resp.
s^{\tt v}=s^{\prime}
).

•

(Normal Forms)
:
t\text{ is a $\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}$-{\tt NF} }
(resp.
\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}
-
NF
) iff
t^{\tt n}
(resp.
t^{\tt v}
) is a
\mathbb{I}
-
NF
.

•

(Simulations)
:
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}u
(resp.
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}}u
) iff
t^{\tt n}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}}u^{\tt n}
(resp.
t^{\tt v}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}}u^{\tt v}
). Moreover, the number of

{\tt dB}/{\tt s}
-steps (resp.
{\tt dB}/{\tt sV}
-steps) on the left matches the
number of
{\tt dB}/{\tt s\oc}
-steps on the right.

Via
Cors.

2
,
4
and
8
, we can project factorization from
dBANG
back to
dCBN
/
dCBV
.

Theorem 5.1
(
dCBN
/
dCBV
Factorizations)

\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}\,=\,\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}
and

\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}}\,=\,\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}}
.

Proof

The proof for
dCBN
is depicted in
Fig.

5
. In particular, since
t^{\tt n}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{S}}s^{\prime}
, one deduces using
Cor.

2

that there exists
s\in\Lambda
such that
s^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}
.

The proof for
dCBV
is depicted in
Fig.

5
. In particular, by construction
u^{\tt v}

is a
\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF
and by induction on the length of
s^{\prime}\rightarrow^{*}_{{\tt I}}u^{\tt v}
, one has that
s^{\prime}
is a

\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF
. Using
Cor.

4
, one deduces
that there exists
s\in\Lambda
such that
s^{\tt v}=s^{\prime}
.
∎

6
Conclusion and Related Work

Our first contribution is to revisit and extend several properties
concerning the encoding of
dCBN
into
dBANG
. The second
contribution,
more significant, consists in introducing a new embedding from

dCBV
to
dBANG
, which is conservative with respect to previous
results in the
literature
[
17
,
18
]
,
but also (and this is a novelty) allows us to establish the essential
reverse simulation property, achieved through the non-trivial concept
of diligent sequence. We illustrate the strength of our methodology by
means of an example, namely factorization. For that, we first prove a
factorization theorem for
dBANG
, another major contribution of the
paper, and we then deduce factorization for
dCBN
and
dCBV
by
projecting that for
dBANG
.

In
[
23
]
, factorization
for the (non-distant) Bang Calculus has been proved and from that,
factorizations results for standard (non distant) CBN

\lambda
-calculus and Plotkin’s original CBV
\lambda
-calculus has
been deduced. But the (non-distant)
Bang Calculus and Plotkin’s CBV are
not
adequate
, in the sense explained in
Sec.

1
, thus decreasing
the significance of those preliminary results.

When taking
adequate
versions of the Bang Calculus, by adding
ES and distance, or
\sigma
-reduction

[
21
,
28
]
, the CBV encodings in
the
literature
[
21
,
27
,
17
,
23
,
18
]

fail to enjoy reverse simulation, thus preventing one deducing dynamic
properties from
the Bang Calculus into CBV. Other CBN and CBV encodings into a
unifying framework appear in
[
13
]
, but there is no
reverse simulation property, so that no concrete application of the
proposed encoding to export properties into CBN and CBV. The same
occurs in
[
22
]
. The only exceptions
are
[
12
,
33
]
—where only

static
properties are obtained—,
and
[
27
,
23
]
—where the
Bang and CBV calculi are not adequate in the sense explained in

Sec.

1
.

Sabry and Wadler
[
46
]
showed that simulation and
reverse simulation between two calculi are for free when their back
and forth translations give rise to an adjoint. One of the
difficulties to achieve our results is that our CBN and CBV
embeddings, as well as the ones used
in
[
21
,
27
,
17
,
23
,
18
]
,
do not form an adjoint. This is basically due to the fact that a
CBN/CBV term can be decorated by
\oc
and
\mathsf{der}
so as that
administrative steps performed in the (Distant) Bang Calculus do not
correspond to anything
in CBN or CBV
. Our contribution is
precisely to achieve simulation and reverse simulation without the
need for any adjoint.

As discussed at the end of
Sec.

3
, proving simulation and reverse simulation requires a
considerable initial effort. However, this investment lays the
groundwork for numerous benefits without extra costs, as we showed
in
Secs.

4
and
5
.

In addition to the tangible contributions presented in this paper, we
believe our methodology enhances the understanding of the semantic
aspects of CBV, especially concerning untyped and typed approximants.
This remains a topic that, while gradually gaining attention in the
literature
[
36
,
30
,
12
]
,
is yet to be thoroughly explored. Our novel CBV embedding would also
suggest a logical counterpart (a new encoding of intuitionistic logic
into linear logic), which remains to be investigated.
Moreover, we aim to further leverage our technique to explore other
crucial dynamic properties of
dCBN
and
dCBV
, such as
standardization, normalization, genericity as well as some specific
deterministic strategies.

Appendix 0.A
Appendix: Proofs of
Sec.

2

0.A.1
Basic Properties

We introduce the following measure, which counts the number of
constructors contained within a term.

Definition 4
(Term Full Size)

\begin{array}[]{rcl c rcl}\left|x\right|_{\mathbb{F}}&:=&0&&\left|t[x\backslash u]\right|_{\mathbb{F}}&:=&1+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|u\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\\
\left|tu\right|_{\mathbb{F}}&:=&1+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|u\right|_{\mathbb{F}}&&\left|\mathsf{der}(t)\right|_{\mathbb{F}}&:=&1+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\\
\left|\lambda x.t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}&:=&1+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}&&\left|\oc t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}&:=&1+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\end{array}

It is extended to all contexts by taking
\left|\diamond\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=0

and show that full term size is compatible with contexts.

Lemma 8

Let
t\in\Lambda_{\oc}
, then for any context
{\tt F}
,

\left|{\tt F}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=\left|{\tt F}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}
.

Proof

By induction on
{\tt F}
. Cases:

{\tt F}=\diamond
: Then
{\tt F}\!\left<t\right>=t
thus

\left|{\tt F}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=0+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=\left|{\tt F}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}
.

{\tt F}={\tt F}^{\prime}\,u
. Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\left|{\tt F}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}&=&\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>u\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;1+\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|u\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\\
&\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{=}&1+\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|u\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}u\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;\left|{\tt F}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\end{array}

{\tt F}=u\,{\tt F}^{\prime}
. Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\left|{\tt F}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}&=&\left|u\,{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;1+\left|u\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\\
&\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{=}&1+\left|u\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;\left|u\,{\tt F}^{\prime}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;\left|{\tt F}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\end{array}

{\tt F}={\tt F}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]
. Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\left|{\tt F}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}&=&\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>[x\backslash u]\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;1+\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|u\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\\
&\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{=}&1+\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|u\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;\left|{\tt F}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\end{array}

{\tt F}=u[x\backslash{\tt F}^{\prime}]
. Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\left|{\tt F}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}&=&\left|u[x\backslash{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>]\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;1+\left|u\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\\
&\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{=}&1+\left|u\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;\left|u[x\backslash{\tt F}^{\prime}]\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;\left|{\tt F}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\end{array}

{\tt F}=\lambda x.{\tt F}^{\prime}
. Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\left|{\tt F}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}&=&\left|\lambda x.{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;1+\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\\
&\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{=}&1+\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;\left|\lambda x.{\tt F}^{\prime}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;\left|{\tt F}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\end{array}

{\tt F}=\mathsf{der}({\tt F}^{\prime})
. Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\left|{\tt F}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}&=&\left|\mathsf{der}({\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>)\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;1+\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\\
&\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{=}&1+\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;\left|\mathsf{der}({\tt F}^{\prime})\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;\left|{\tt F}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\end{array}

{\tt F}=\oc{\tt F}^{\prime}
. Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\left|{\tt F}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}&=&\left|\oc{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;1+\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\\
&\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{=}&1+\left|{\tt F}^{\prime}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;\left|\oc{\tt F}^{\prime}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\;=\;\left|{\tt F}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}\end{array}

∎

We now use this measure to prove the termination of reductions

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>},\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
.

Lemma 9

Reductions
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
and
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
are
terminating.

Proof

To prove that
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
is terminating, it is enough to show
that
\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}>\left|u\right|_{\mathbb{F}}
for any
t,u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u
. By definition, there
are
t^{\prime},u^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
and a contexts
{\tt F}
such that

t={\tt F}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>
,
u={\tt F}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>
with
t^{\prime}\mapsto_{{\tt d\oc}}u^{\prime}
. So,
t^{\prime}=\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s\right>)
and
u^{\prime}={\tt L}\!\left<s\right>
for
some
s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
and list context
{\tt L}
. Using

Lem.

8
, one deduces that

\left|t^{\prime}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=\left|\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s\right>)\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=2+\left|s\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|{\tt L}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}>\left|s\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|{\tt L}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=\left|{\tt L}\!\left<s\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=\left|u^{\prime}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}
, and using
Lem.

8

again we conclude that
\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=\left|{\tt F}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}>\left|{\tt F}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=\left|u\right|_{\mathbb{F}}
.

As
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\,\subseteq\,\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
, termination of

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
implies termination of
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
.
∎

Lemma 10

Reductions
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}
and
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}
are
terminating.

Proof

To prove that
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}
is terminating, it is enough to show
that
\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}>\left|u\right|_{\mathbb{F}}
for any
t,u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}u
. By definition, there
are
t^{\prime},u^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
and a context
{\tt F}
such that
t={\tt F}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>
,
u={\tt F}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>
with
t^{\prime}\mapsto_{{\tt dB}}u^{\prime}
.
So,
t^{\prime}={\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.s\right>r
and
u^{\prime}={\tt L}\!\left<s[x\backslash r]\right>
for some
s,r\in\Lambda_{\oc}
and list
context
{\tt L}
. By
Lem.

8
, one has

\left|t^{\prime}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=\left|{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.s\right>r\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=2+\left|s\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|r\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|{\tt L}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}>1+\left|s\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|r\right|_{\mathbb{F}}+\left|{\tt L}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=\left|{\tt L}\!\left<s[x\backslash r]\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=\left|u^{\prime}\right|_{\mathbb{F}}
, and
using
Lem.

8
again we conclude that

\left|t\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=\left|{\tt F}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}>\left|{\tt F}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right|_{\mathbb{F}}=\left|u\right|_{\mathbb{F}}
.

As
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\,\subseteq\,\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}
, termination of

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}
implies termination of
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}
.
∎

0.A.2
Abstract Diligence for
dBANG

In this subsection we introduce an abstract proof of diligence
process. The proof is parametrized using a family
\mathbb{E}
of

dBANG
contexts.

We first introduce a parametric definition of administrive diligent
reduction.

Definition 5
(Administrative Diligent Reduction)

The
administrative diligent reduction

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}

associated with a family
\mathbb{E}\subseteq\mathbb{F}

of contexts is a subset of the reduction
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}}
obtained
by restricting
{\tt dB}
and
{\tt s\oc}
-steps to

\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>
-normal forms. More precisely, it is
defined as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}&\coloneqq&(\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\cap\;\text{$\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!$-{\tt NF} }\times\Lambda_{\oc})\;\;\cup\;\;(\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\cap\;\text{$\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!$-{\tt NF} }\times\Lambda_{\oc})\;\;\cup\;\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\end{array}

We say that the reduction
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}}

admits a diligence
process
if
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
whenever
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}}u

with
u
a
\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF
.

Lemma 11
(
\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>
-Head Steps Extraction)

Let
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
, then
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u

or there is a
\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF

s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that

t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}s
and
s\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
.

Proof

By induction on the length of the reduction sequence
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
. Cases:

t\rightarrow^{0}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
: Then
t=u
thus in
particular
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u
concluding this case.

t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}s_{1}\rightarrow^{n}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
: We
distinguish
2
cases:

t
is a
\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF
: Thus taking
s=t

concludes this case since by reflexivity
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}s
and by hypothesis
s\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
.

t
is not a
\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF
: Then necessarily
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}s_{1}\rightarrow^{n}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
. By
i.h.
on
s_{1}\rightarrow^{n}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
, two cases can be distinguished:

\bullet

s_{1}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u
: Then
t\rightarrow^{*}_{{\tt E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u
concluding this case.

\bullet

s_{1}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}s
and
s\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
for some
\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF

s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
: Then
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}s
and
s\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
concluding this case.
∎

Lemma 12

Let
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s\oc}\}
. Suppose that for any

t,u_{1},u_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that

t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}u_{1}
and
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{2}
, there is
s\in\Lambda_{\oc}

making the diagram below commute.

\begin{array}[]{ccc}t&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&u_{1}\\
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&&{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}\\
u_{2}&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&s\end{array}

Then, for any
t,u_{1},u_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that

t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}u_{1}
and
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{2}
, there exists
s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that the following diagram also commutes.

\begin{array}[]{ccc}t&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&u_{1}\\
{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}&&{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}\\
u_{2}&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&s\end{array}

Proof

By induction on the length of the derivation
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{2}
. Cases:

t=u_{2}
: One trivially concludes by taking
s=u_{1}
.

t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{2}
: We
consider the diagram below. By hypothesis, there is
u_{1}^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that the top square commutes. By
i.h.
, there is
s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that the bottom square commutes.

\begin{array}[]{ccc}t&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&u_{1}\\
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\\
u^{\prime}_{2}&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&u^{\prime}_{1}\\
{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}&&{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}\\
u_{2}&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&s\end{array}

∎

Lemma 13
(One Step Abstract Diligence)

Let
\mathbb{E}\subseteq\mathbb{F}
be a family of contexts
such that:

the reduction relation
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}

is confluent;

for any
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s\oc}\}
and any

t,u_{1},u_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that

t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}u_{1}
and
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{2}
, there exists
s\in\Lambda_{\oc}

making the following diagram commutes.

\begin{array}[]{ccc}t&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&u_{1}\\
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&&{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}\\
u_{2}&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&s\end{array}

If
t,u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
where
u
is a
\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF
and
such that
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u
, then
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
.

Proof

Let
t,u,s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
with
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}}s\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u
where
u
is a
\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF
. Two cases:

t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}s
with
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s\oc}\}
. Consider the diagram below. Since

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
terminates
(
Lem.

9
), there is a
\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF

t^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}t^{\prime}
. Using

Lem.

12
there is
s^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that the
left square commutes. By confluence of
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
there
is
u^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that the right square commutes.

\begin{array}[]{clclc}t&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&s&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&u\\
{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}&&{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}&&{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}\\
t^{\prime}&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&s^{\prime}&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&u^{\prime}\end{array}

As
u
is
\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF
, necessarily
u=u^{\prime}
.
As
\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\subseteq\rightarrow^{*}_{{\tt E}{\tt ad}}
, one has
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}t^{\prime}
and
s^{\prime}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
.
Since
t^{\prime}
is

\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF
, then
t^{\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}s^{\prime}
and so
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
by transitivity.

t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u
: Then
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u
, and so
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
, since

\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\subseteq\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}
.
∎

Lemma 14

Let
\mathbb{E}
be a family of contexts such that:

The reduction relation
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
is confluent.

For any
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s\oc}\}
and any

t,u_{1},u_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that

t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}u_{1}
and
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{2}
, there exists
s\in\Lambda_{\oc}

making the following diagram commutes:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}t&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&u_{1}\\
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&&{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}\\
u_{2}&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&s\end{array}

Then the reduction relation
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}}
admits a diligence
process.

Proof

Let
t,u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
and
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}}u
such that
u
is
a
\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF
. We show that
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
.
We proceed by induction on the length of the derivation
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}}u
:

t=u
: Then by reflexivity
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
.

t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}}s\rightarrow^{n}_{\mathbb{E}}u
for some
s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
: By
i.h.
on
s\rightarrow^{n}_{\mathbb{E}}u
, one obtains
s\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
. Using
Lem.

11
, two cases can
be distinguished:

s\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u
: Then
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u
and hence, by
Lem.

13
,
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
.

s\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}s^{\prime}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
for
some
\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF

s^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
: Then
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}s^{\prime}
and so
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}s^{\prime}
by
Lem.

13
, hence
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}{\tt ad}}u
by
transitivity.
∎

See
1

Proof

•

(Surface)
See
Sec.

0.A.3
, in particular

Lem.

17
.

•

(Full)
See
Sec.

0.A.4
, in particular

Lem.

21
.
∎

Lemma 16
(Diamond of
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
)

Let
t,u_{1},u_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
with
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{1}
,
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{2}
and
u_{1}\neq u_{2}
. Then there is

s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that the diagram below commutes.

\begin{array}[]{ccc}t&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&u_{1}\\
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\\
u_{2}&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&s\end{array}

Proof

See
[
18
, Lemma 2.4]
, taking
p_{1}=p_{2}={\tt d\oc}
.
∎

Corollary 9
(Confluence of
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
)

Let
t,u_{1},u_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
with
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{1}
and
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{2}
. Then there exists
s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that the diagram below commutes.

\begin{array}[]{ccc}t&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}^{*}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&u_{1}\\
{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}&&{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}\\
u_{2}&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}^{*}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&s\end{array}

Proof

Immediate consequence of the diamond for
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}

(
Lem.

16
).
∎

Lemma 17
(Surface Diligence)

Let
t,u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{S}}u
. If

u
is a
\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF
, then
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{S}{\tt ad}}u
.

Proof

Just apply
Lem.

14
to
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}}
,
since its hypotheses are fulfilled
(
Cors.

9
and
15
).
∎

0.A.4
Full Diligence for
dBANG

Definition 6

Full reduction

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}
is extended to contexts
as expected:

•

either the redex occurs in a
subterm
of some context,

•

or the redex occurs in a
subcontext
, where, in particular,

{\tt s\oc}
-redexes are only defined
as

t[x\backslash(\oc u)[x_{1}\backslash u_{1}]\ldots[x_{i}\backslash{\tt F}_{i}]\ldots[x_{n}\backslash u_{n}]]
, but not as
t[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc{\tt F}\right>]
or
{\tt F}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc u\right>]
.

Examples of the first case are
\diamond[x\backslash(\lambda w.w)z]\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\diamond[x\backslash w[w\backslash z]]
,
\diamond\,\mathsf{der}(\oc t)\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\diamond\,t
and
\oc(x[x\backslash\oc y])\diamond\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\oc(y)\diamond
, while examples of the
second case are
(\lambda x.x){\diamond}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}x[x\backslash\diamond]
and
\mathsf{der}(\oc\diamond)\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\diamond
but
\diamond[x\backslash z]

but
x[x\backslash\oc\diamond]

are
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}
-irreducible.

Lemma 18

If
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}t^{\prime}
and
{\tt F}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}{\tt F}^{\prime}
, then

1.

t\{x\backslash u\}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}t^{\prime}\{x\backslash u\}
;

2.

u\{x\backslash t\}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}u\{x\backslash t^{\prime}\}
;

3.

{\tt F}\!\left<t\right>\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}{\tt F}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>
;

4.

{\tt F}\!\left<t\right>\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>
.

Proof

1.

By straightforward induction on the definition of
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}t^{\prime}
.

2.

By straightforward induction on
u
.

3.

Since
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}t^{\prime}
, there is a full context

{\tt F}^{\prime}
and
r,r^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t={\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<r\right>\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<r^{\prime}\right>=t^{\prime}
with
r\mapsto_{\mathcal{R}}r^{\prime}
. As
{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt F}^{\prime}\right>
is a full context,

{\tt F}\!\left<t\right>={\tt F}\!\left<{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<r\right>\right>\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<r^{\prime}\right>\right>={\tt F}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>
.

4.

By straightforward induction on
{\tt F}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}{\tt F}^{\prime}
. ∎

Lemma 19

Let
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s\oc}\}
and
t,u_{1},u_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{1}
and
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}u_{2}
. Then there exists
s\in\Lambda_{\oc}

such that the diagram below commutes.

\begin{array}[]{ccc}t&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&u_{2}\\
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}\\
u_{1}&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&s\end{array}

Proof

We analyze different (potentially overlapping) situations that at the
end cover all possible cases. To close the diagrams we
use
Lem.

18
.

1.

t={\tt F}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>
and
u_{1}={\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>
and the step
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}u_{2}
occurs inside
{\tt F}
,
{\tt L}
, or
s_{1}
. We
analyze all the possible cases.

If
{\tt F}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}{\tt F}^{\prime}
, then:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt F}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>\end{array}

If
{\tt L}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}{\tt L}^{\prime}
,
then:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt F}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>\end{array}

If
s_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}s^{\prime}_{1}
, then:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt F}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s^{\prime}_{1}\right>)\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{1}\right>\right>\end{array}

2.

t={\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>s_{2}\right>
and

u_{2}={\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right>\right>
and the step
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{1}
occurs inside
{\tt F}
,
{\tt L}
,

s_{1}
, or
s_{2}
. We analyze all the possible cases.

If
{\tt F}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}{\tt F}^{\prime}
, then:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>s_{2}\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right>\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>s_{2}\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right>\right>\end{array}

If
{\tt L}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}{\tt L}^{\prime}
, then:

\hskip-42.67912pt\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>s_{2}\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right>\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>s_{2}\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right>\right>\end{array}

If
s_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}s^{\prime}_{1}
, then:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>s_{2}\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right>\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.s^{\prime}_{1}\right>s_{2}\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right>\right>\end{array}

If
s_{2}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}s^{\prime}_{2}
, then:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>s_{2}\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right>\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>s^{\prime}_{2}\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash s^{\prime}_{2}]\right>\right>\end{array}

3.

t={\tt F}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{2}\right>]\right>
and
u_{2}={\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\{x\backslash s_{2}\}\right>\right>
and the step
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{1}
occurs inside
{\tt F}
,
{\tt L}
,

s_{1}
or
s_{2}
. We analyze all the possible cases.

If
{\tt F}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}{\tt F}^{\prime}
, then:

\hskip-28.45274pt\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt F}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{2}\right>]\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\{x\backslash s_{2}\}\right>\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{2}\right>]\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\{x\backslash s_{2}\}\right>\right>\end{array}

If
s_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}s^{\prime}_{1}
, then:

\hskip-28.45274pt\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt F}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{2}\right>]\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\{x\backslash s_{2}\}\right>\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt F}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{2}\right>]\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{1}\{x\backslash s_{2}\}\right>\right>\end{array}

If
{\tt L}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}{\tt L}^{\prime}
, then:

\hskip-28.45274pt\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt F}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{2}\right>]\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\{x\backslash s_{2}\}\right>\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt F}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc s_{2}\right>]\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{1}\{x\backslash s_{2}\}\right>\right>\end{array}

If
s_{2}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}s^{\prime}_{2}
, then:

\hskip-28.45274pt\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt F}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{2}\right>]\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\{x\backslash s_{2}\}\right>\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt F}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc s^{\prime}_{2}\right>]\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\{x\backslash s^{\prime}_{2}\}\right>\right>\end{array}

∎

Lemma 20
(Diamond of
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
)

Let
t,u_{1},u_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
.
If
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{1}
,
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{2}
and
u_{1}\neq u_{2}
, then there is

s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that the diagram below commutes.

\begin{array}[]{ccc}t&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&u_{2}\\
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\
u_{1}&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&s\end{array}

Proof

As
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{2}
, then
t={\tt F}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>
and
u_{2}={\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>
. Different cases have
to be distinguished, since the reduction step
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{1}
may occur inside
{\tt F}
,
{\tt L}
, or
s_{1}
; in all
these cases we use
Lem.

18
to conclude:

If
{\tt F}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}{\tt F}^{\prime}
, then:

\hskip-28.45274pt\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt F}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>\end{array}

If
{\tt L}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}{\tt L}^{\prime}
, then:

\hskip-28.45274pt\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt F}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt F}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>\end{array}

If
s_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}s^{\prime}_{1}
, then:

\hskip-28.45274pt\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt F}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt F}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s^{\prime}_{1}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{1}\right>\right>\end{array}

Corollary 10
(Confluence of
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
)

Let
t,u_{1},u_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{1}
and
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{2}
.
Then there is
s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that the diagram below commutes.

\begin{array}[]{ccc}t&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}^{*}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&u_{1}\\
{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}&&{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}\\
u_{2}&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}^{*}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&s\end{array}

Proof

Immediate consequence of the diamond for
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}

(
Lem.

20
).
∎

Lemma 21
(Full Diligence)

Let
t,u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{F}}u
. If

u
is a
\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF
, then
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{F}{\tt ad}}u
.

Proof

Just apply
Lem.

14
to
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}}
,
since its hypotheses are fulfilled
(
Cors.

10
and
19
).
∎

Appendix 0.B
Appendix: Proofs of
Sec.

3

0.B.1
The Call-by-Name Calculus
dCBN

Lemma 22

Let
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
and
t\in\Lambda
, then

({\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\!\left<t\right>)^{\tt n}={\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\!\left<t^{\tt n}\right>
.

Proof

By straightforward induction on
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
.
∎

See
2

Proof

By definition, there exist
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
and
t^{\prime},u^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such that
t={\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>
,
u={\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>
and
t^{\prime}\mapsto_{\mathcal{R}}u^{\prime}
for some
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s}\}
. We distinguish two cases on

\mathcal{R}
:

\mathcal{R}={\tt dB}
: Then there are
s_{1},s_{2}\in\Lambda
and
{\tt L}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{L}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that
t^{\prime}={\tt L}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>s_{2}
and
s^{\prime}={\tt L}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right>
. By
Lem.

22
,
{t^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt L}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}^{\tt n}\right>\oc s_{2}^{\tt n}\mapsto_{{\tt dB}^{\tt n}}{\tt L}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\!\left<s_{1}^{\tt n}[x\backslash\oc s_{2}^{\tt n}]\right>={u^{\prime}}^{\tt n}
so that using
Lem.

22
again,
t^{\tt n}={\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\!\left<{t^{\prime}}^{\tt n}\right>\rightarrow_{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\!\left<{\tt dB}^{\tt n}\right>}{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\!\left<{u^{\prime}}^{\tt n}\right>=u^{\tt n}
.

\mathcal{R}={\tt s}
: Then there are
s_{1},s_{2}\in\Lambda
such that
t^{\prime}=s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]
and
s^{\prime}=s_{1}\{x\backslash s_{2}\}
. By induction on
s_{1}
, one has that

{u^{\prime}}^{\tt n}=s_{1}^{\tt n}\{x\backslash s_{2}^{\tt n}\}
thus

{t^{\prime}}^{\tt n}=s_{1}^{\tt n}[x\backslash\oc s_{2}^{\tt n}]\mapsto_{{\tt s}^{\tt n}}s_{1}^{\tt n}\{x\backslash s_{2}^{\tt n}\}={u^{\prime}}^{\tt n}
so that
t^{\tt n}={\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\!\left<{t^{\prime}}^{\tt n}\right>\rightarrow_{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\!\left<{\tt s}^{\tt n}\right>}{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\!\left<{u^{\prime}}^{\tt n}\right>=u^{\tt n}
by
Lem.

22
.
∎

Lemma 23

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
, then
(t\{x\backslash u\})^{\tt n}=t^{\tt n}\{x\backslash u^{\tt n}\}
.

Proof

By induction on
t\in\Lambda
.

See
3

Proof

Let
t\in\Lambda
,
s^{\prime}_{t},s^{\prime}_{u}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s\oc},{\tt d\oc}\}
. Let us show the
following property by induction on
{\tt F}\in\mathbb{F}
:

<< For all
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that
t^{\tt n}={\tt F}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{t}\right>
and
s^{\prime}_{t}\mapsto_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}}s^{\prime}_{u}
, there exist
s_{t},s_{u}\in\Lambda
,
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s}\}
and

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that
s_{t}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{t}
,

s_{u}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{u}
,
s_{t}\mapsto_{\mathcal{R}}s_{u}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\tt n}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
and
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}={\tt F}
. >>

{\tt F}=\diamond
: Let
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=\diamond
, then

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}={\tt F}
. We distinguish three cases on

\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
:

\mathcal{R}={\tt dB}
: Then
s^{\prime}_{t}={\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.s^{\prime}_{1}\right>s^{\prime}_{2}
and
s^{\prime}_{u}={\tt L}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash s^{\prime}_{2}]\right>
for some
{\tt L}\in\mathbb{L}
and
s^{\prime}_{1},s^{\prime}_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
. Since

t^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{t}
, then there exists
{\tt L}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{L}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
and
s_{1},s_{2}\in\Lambda
such that

{\tt L}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}={\tt L}
,
s_{1}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{1}
and
\oc s_{2}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{2}
. Taking
s_{t}={\tt L}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>s_{2}
,
s_{u}={\tt L}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right>
and
\mathcal{R}={\tt dB}

concludes this case since using
Lem.

22
:

\begin{array}[]{c}s_{t}^{\tt n}\;=\;{\tt L}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}^{\tt n}\right>\oc s_{2}^{\tt n}\;=\;{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.s^{\prime}_{1}\right>s^{\prime}_{2}\;=\;s^{\prime}_{t}\\
s_{u}^{\tt n}\;=\;{\tt L}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\!\left<s_{1}^{\tt n}[x\backslash\oc s_{2}^{\tt n}]\right>\;=\;{\tt L}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash s^{\prime}_{2}]\right>\;=\;s^{\prime}_{u}\\
s_{t}\;=\;{\tt L}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>s_{2}\;\mapsto_{\mathcal{R}}\;{\tt L}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right>\;=\;s_{u}\\
\mathcal{R}^{\tt n}\;=\;{\tt dB}^{\tt n}\;=\;{\tt dB}\;=\;\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\end{array}

\mathcal{R}={\tt s\oc}
: Then
s^{\prime}_{t}=s^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash s^{\prime}_{2}]
and
s^{\prime}_{u}=s^{\prime}_{1}\{x\backslash s^{\prime}_{2}\}
for some

s^{\prime}_{1},s^{\prime}_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
. Since
t^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{t}
, then there exists
s_{1},s_{2}\in\Lambda
such that

s_{1}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{1}
and
\oc s_{2}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{2}
. Taking
s_{t}=s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]
,
s_{u}=s_{1}\{x\backslash s_{2}\}
and
\mathcal{R}={\tt s}
concludes this
case since:

\begin{array}[]{c}s_{t}^{\tt n}\;=\;s_{1}^{\tt n}[x\backslash\oc s_{2}^{\tt n}]\;=\;s^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash s^{\prime}_{2}]\;=\;s^{\prime}_{t}\\
s_{u}^{\tt n}\;\hbox{\hskip 12.5556pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{=}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-12.5556pt\raisebox{9.16875pt}{\hbox{{\scriptsize Lem.\ref{lem: cbn(t{x:=u}) = cbn(t){x:=cbn(u)}}}}}\hskip-12.5556pt\hskip 12.5556pt}\;s_{1}^{\tt n}\{x\backslash\oc s_{2}^{\tt n}\}\;=\;s^{\prime}_{1}\{x\backslash s^{\prime}_{2}\}\;=\;s^{\prime}_{u}\\
s_{t}\;=\;s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\;\mapsto_{\mathcal{R}}\;s_{1}\{x\backslash s_{2}\}\;=\;s_{u}\\
\mathcal{R}^{\tt n}\;=\;{\tt s}^{\tt n}\;=\;{\tt s\oc}\;=\;\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\end{array}

\mathcal{R}={\tt d\oc}
: Then
t^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{t}=\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s\right>)
for some
{\tt L}\in\mathbb{L}
and
s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
which is impossible
by definition of the embedding.

{\tt F}=\lambda x.{\tt F}^{\prime}
: Then

t^{\tt n}=\lambda x.{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{t}\right>
thus necessarily

t=\lambda x.t^{\prime}
for some
t^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such that

{t^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>
. By induction on

{\tt F}^{\prime}
, there exist
s_{t},s_{u}\in\Lambda
,
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s}\}
and
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}

such that
s_{t}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{t}
,
s_{u}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{u}
,
s_{t}\mapsto_{\mathcal{R}}s_{u}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\tt n}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
and

{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt F}^{\prime}
. Taking
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=\lambda x.{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}
concludes this case since

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}=\lambda x.{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}=\lambda x.{\tt F}^{\prime}={\tt F}
.

{\tt F}={\tt F}^{\prime}s^{\prime}
: Then

t^{\tt n}={\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{t}\right>s^{\prime}
thus necessarily

t=t^{\prime}s
for some
t^{\prime},s\in\Lambda
such that

t^{\prime\tt n}={\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{t}\right>
and
\oc s^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}
. By induction on
{\tt F}^{\prime}
, there exist
s_{t},s_{u}\in\Lambda
,
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s}\}
and

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that
s_{t}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{t}
,
s_{u}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{u}
,
s_{t}\mapsto_{\mathcal{R}}s_{u}
,

\mathcal{R}^{\tt n}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
and
{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt F}^{\prime}
. Taking
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}={\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\,s

concludes this case since
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}={{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}\oc s^{\tt n}={\tt F}^{\prime}s^{\prime}={\tt F}
.

{\tt F}=s^{\prime}\,{\tt F}^{\prime}
: Then

t^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}\,{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{t}\right>
thus
necessarily
t=s\,t^{\prime}
for some
s,t^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such that
s^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}
and

\oc t^{\prime\tt n}={\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{t}\right>
. Therefore

{\tt F}^{\prime\prime}=\oc{\tt F}^{\prime\prime}
for some
{\tt F}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{F}
such that
t^{\prime\tt n}={\tt F}^{\prime\prime}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{t}\right>
.
By induction on
{\tt F}^{\prime\prime}
, there exist
s_{t},s_{u}\in\Lambda
,
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s}\}
and

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that
s_{t}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{t}
,
s_{u}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{u}
,
s_{t}\mapsto_{\mathcal{R}}s_{u}
,

\mathcal{R}^{\tt n}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
and
{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt F}^{\prime\prime}
. Taking
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=s\,{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}

concludes this case since
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}=s^{\tt n}\oc{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}\,\oc{\tt F}^{\prime\prime}=s^{\prime}\,{\tt F}^{\prime}={\tt F}
.

{\tt F}={\tt F}^{\prime}[x\backslash s^{\prime}]
: Then

t^{\tt n}={\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{t}\right>[x\backslash s^{\prime}]
thus
necessarily
t=t^{\prime}[x\backslash s]
for some
t^{\prime},s\in\Lambda

such that
t^{\prime\tt n}={\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{t}\right>
and

\oc s^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}
. By induction on
{\tt F}^{\prime}
, there
exist
s_{t},s_{u}\in\Lambda
,
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s}\}
and
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that

s_{t}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{t}
,
s_{u}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{u}
,
s_{t}\mapsto_{\mathcal{R}}s_{u}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\tt n}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
and

{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt F}^{\prime}
. Taking
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}={\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}[x\backslash s]
concludes this case since

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}={{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}[x\backslash\oc s^{\tt n}]={\tt F}^{\prime}[x\backslash s^{\prime}]={\tt F}
.

{\tt F}=s^{\prime}[x\backslash{\tt F}^{\prime}]
: Then

t^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}[x\backslash{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{t}\right>]
thus
necessarily
t=s[x\backslash t^{\prime}]
for some
s,t^{\prime}\in\Lambda

such that
s^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}
and
\oc t^{\prime\tt n}={\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{t}\right>
. Therefore
{\tt F}^{\prime\prime}=\oc{\tt F}^{\prime\prime}
for
some
{\tt F}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{F}
such that
t^{\prime\tt n}={\tt F}^{\prime\prime}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{t}\right>
. By induction on
{\tt F}^{\prime\prime}
, there exist

s_{t},s_{u}\in\Lambda
,
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s}\}
and
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that

s_{t}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{t}
,
s_{u}^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}_{u}
,
s_{t}\mapsto_{\mathcal{R}}s_{u}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\tt n}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
and

{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt F}^{\prime\prime}
. Taking
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=s[x\backslash{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}]
concludes this case since

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}=s^{\tt n}[x\backslash\oc{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}]=s^{\prime}[x\backslash\oc{\tt F}^{\prime\prime}]=s^{\prime}[x\backslash{\tt F}^{\prime}]={\tt F}
.

{\tt F}=\mathsf{der}({\tt F}^{\prime})
: Then
t^{\tt n}=\mathsf{der}({\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{t}\right>)
which is impossible by definition of

\cdot^{\tt n}
.

{\tt F}=\oc{\tt F}^{\prime}
: Then
t^{\tt n}=\oc{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{t}\right>
which is impossible by definition of

\cdot^{\tt n}
.

Lemma 24

Let
\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
,
\mathbb{E}\subseteq\mathbb{F}
be two families of contexts such that:

1.

Let
{\tt E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
, then

{\tt E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\in\mathbb{E}
.

2.

Let
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
, if there exists

{\tt E}\in\mathbb{E}
such that

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}={\tt E}
, then
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
.

Then, the following properties hold:

(Normal Forms)
:
\forall\,t\in\Lambda,

t\text{ is a $\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}$-{\tt NF} }\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad t^{\tt n}\text{ is a $\mathbb{E}$-{\tt NF} }

(Stability)
:
\forall\,t,\in\Lambda,\;\forall\,u\in\Lambda_{\oc},

t^{\tt n}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\prime}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\exists\,u\in\Lambda,\;u^{\tt n}=u^{\prime}

(Simulation and Reverse Simulation)
:

\forall\,t,u\in\Lambda,

t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}u\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad t^{\tt n}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\tt n}

Moreover, the number of
{\tt dB}/{\tt s}
-steps
matches the number
{\tt dB}/{\tt s\oc}
-steps.

Proof

Let us first show that the following two properties hold:

(One Step Simulation)
:
\forall\,t,u\in\Lambda,

t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}u\quad\Rightarrow\quad t^{\tt n}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\tt n}

(One Step Reverse Simulation)
:
\forall\,t,\in\Lambda,\;\forall\,u^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc},

t^{\tt n}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\prime}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\exists\,u\in\Lambda,\;t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}}u\text{ and }u^{\tt n}=u^{\prime}

In particular,
{\tt dB}
-steps (resp.
{\tt s}
-steps) are
simulated by
{\tt dB}
-steps (resp.
{\tt s\oc}
-steps).

We distinguish the two cases:

(One Step Simulation)
: Let
t,u\in\Lambda
such that
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}u
. Thus
t\rightarrow_{{\tt E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}u
for some
{\tt E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
and
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s}\}
. Since
\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
, then
t^{\tt n}\rightarrow_{{\tt E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\!\left<\mathcal{R}^{\tt n}\right>}u^{\tt n}
using one step full simulation
(
Lem.

2
). Since
{\tt E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
, then by hypothesis
{\tt E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\in{\tt E}
and one finally concludes that
t^{\tt n}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\tt n}
.

(One Step Reverse Simulation)
: Let
t\in\Lambda
and
u^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that

t^{\tt n}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\prime}
. Then
t^{\tt n}\rightarrow_{{\tt E}\!\left<\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\right>}u^{\prime}
for some
{\tt E}\in\mathbb{E}
and

\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s\oc}\}
. Since
\mathbb{E}\subseteq\mathbb{F}
, then using one step full reverse
simulation (
Lem.

3
) there exists
u\in\Lambda
such that
u^{\tt n}=u^{\prime}
,
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
with
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}={\tt E}
,
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s}\}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\tt n}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}

and
t\rightarrow_{{\tt E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}u
. Since
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\in\mathbb{E}
, then by hypothesis
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
so
that
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}u
.

Using these, let us now prove the three expected properties:

(Normal Forms)
: Direct consequence of simulation
and reverse simulation.

(Stability)
: Let
t\in\Lambda
and
u^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t^{\tt n}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\prime}
.
Let us proceed by induction on the length of the derivation

t^{\tt n}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\prime}
. Cases:

t^{\tt n}\rightarrow^{0}_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\prime}
: Then
u^{\prime}=t^{\tt n}
and taking
u=t
trivially concludes this
case.

t^{\tt n}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}}s^{\prime}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\prime}
: Using one step reverse simulation, there exists
s\in\Lambda
such that
s^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}
and thus by

i.h.
on
s^{\tt n}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\prime}
, one concludes
that there exists
u\in\Lambda
such that

u^{\tt n}=u^{\prime}
.

(Simulation)
: Let
t,u\in\Lambda
such
that
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}u
. We proceed by induction on the length of
the derivation
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}u
. Cases:

t\rightarrow^{0}_{\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}u
: Then
t=u
thus

t^{\tt n}=u^{\tt n}
and by reflexivity

t^{\tt n}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\tt n}
.

t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}s\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}u
: On one hand

t^{\tt n}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}s^{\tt n}
using one
step simulation. On the other hand, by
i.h.
on
s\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}u
, one has that
s^{\tt n}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\tt n}
. Finally, one concludes by transitivity that

t^{\tt n}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\tt n}
.

(Reverse Simulation)
: Let
t,u\in\Lambda
such that
t^{\tt n}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\tt n}
. We proceed by induction on the length of the
derivation
t^{\tt n}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\tt n}
.
Cases:

t^{\tt n}\rightarrow^{0}_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\tt n}
:
Then
t^{\tt n}=u^{\tt n}
thus by injectivity
of
(\cdot)^{\tt n}

t=u
and by reflexivity
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}u
.

t^{\tt n}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}}s^{\prime}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\tt n}
: On one hand, using one step reverse
simulation, there exists
s\in\Lambda
such that
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}s
with
s^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}
. On the other
hand, by
i.h.
on
s^{\tt n}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}}u^{\tt n}
, one has that
s\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}u
. Finally,
one concludes by transitivity that
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{E}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}}u
.
∎

Lemma 25

Let
t\in\Lambda
, then:

1.

(
dCBN

\rightarrow

dBANG
)
Let
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
, then

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\in\mathbb{F}
.

2.

(
dBANG

\rightarrow

dCBN
)
Let
{\tt F}\in\mathbb{F}
and
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}={\tt F}
, then
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
.

Proof

1.

(
dCBN

\rightarrow

dBANG
)
By definition of

\cdot^{\tt n}
.

2.

(
dBANG

\rightarrow

dCBN
)
By hypothesis.

See
1

Proof

Using
Lem.

24
and

Lem.

25
.

See

Proof

1.

(
dCBN

\rightarrow

dBANG
)
By induction on

{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
:

{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=\diamond
: Then
{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}=\diamond^{\tt n}=\diamond\in\mathbb{S}
.

{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=\lambda x.{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}
: By
i.h.
,

{{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}\in\mathbb{S}
thus

{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}=\lambda x.{{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}\in\lambda x.\mathbb{S}\subseteq\mathbb{S}
.

{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}={\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\,s
: By
i.h.
on

{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}
, one has that
{{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}\in\mathbb{S}
which concludes this case since

{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}={{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}\,\oc s^{\tt n}\in\mathbb{S}\,\oc s^{\tt n}\subseteq\mathbb{S}
.

{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}={\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}[x\backslash s]
: By
i.h.
on

{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}
, one has that
{{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}\in\mathbb{S}
which concludes this case since

{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}={{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}[x\backslash\oc s^{\tt n}]\in\mathbb{S}[x\backslash\oc s^{\tt n}]\subseteq\mathbb{S}
.

2.

(
dBANG

\rightarrow

dCBN
)
Let
{\tt S}\in\mathbb{S}
and
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}={\tt S}
.
By induction on
{\tt S}\in\mathbb{S}
:

{\tt S}=\diamond
: Then necessarily
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=\diamond
thus
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
.

{\tt S}=\lambda x.{\tt S}^{\prime}
: Then necessarily

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=\lambda x.{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}
for some
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that
{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt S}^{\prime}
. By
i.h.
on
{\tt S}^{\prime}
, one has that

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
so that
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=\lambda x.{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\lambda x.\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\subseteq\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
.

{\tt S}={\tt S}^{\prime}\,s^{\prime}
: Then
necessarily
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}={\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\,s
for some

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
and
s\in\Lambda
such
that
{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt S}^{\prime}
and

\oc s^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}
. By
i.h.
on
{\tt S}^{\prime}
, one has
that
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
so that
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}={\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\,s\in\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\,s\subseteq\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
.

{\tt S}=s^{\prime}\,{\tt S}
: Then
necessarily
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=s\,{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}
for some
s\in\Lambda
and
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that

s^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}
and
\oc{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt S}
which is impossible since no
{\tt S}\in\mathbb{S}
can have a hole under a bang.

{\tt S}={\tt S}^{\prime}[x\backslash s^{\prime}]
: Then
necessarily
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}={\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}[x\backslash s]
for some

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
and
s\in\Lambda
such
that
{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt S}^{\prime}
and

\oc s^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}
. By
i.h.
on
{\tt S}^{\prime}
, one has
that
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
so that
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}={\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}[x\backslash s]\in\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}[x\backslash s]\subseteq\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
.

{\tt S}=s^{\prime}[x\backslash{\tt S}^{\prime}]
: Then
necessarily
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=s[x\backslash{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}]
for some
s\in\Lambda
and
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that

s^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}
and
\oc{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt S}
which is impossible since no
{\tt S}\in\mathbb{S}
can have a hole under a bang.

{\tt S}=\mathsf{der}({\tt S}^{\prime})
: Impossible since
one cannot have
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}=\mathsf{der}({\tt S})

by definition of the
dCBN
embedding on contexts.
