Lemma 41
(Diamond of
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
)

Let
t,u_{1},u_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
.
If
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{1}
,
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{2}
and
u_{1}\neq u_{2}
, then there is

s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that the diagram below commutes.

\begin{array}[]{ccc}t&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&u_{2}\\
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\
u_{1}&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&s\end{array}

Proof

As
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{2}
, then
t={\tt I}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>
and
u_{2}={\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>
. Different cases have
to be distinguished, since the reduction step
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{1}
may occur internally inside
{\tt I}
, or fully inside

{\tt L}
, or
s_{1}
; in all these cases we use

Lems.

18
and
39
to conclude.

If
{\tt I}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}{\tt F}
, then we
know from
Lem.

39
.1 that there exist

{\tt I}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{I}
such that
{\tt F}={\tt I}^{\prime}
. Then:

\hskip-28.45274pt\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt I}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt I}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt I}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>\end{array}

If
{\tt L}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}{\tt L}^{\prime}
, then:

\hskip-28.45274pt\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt I}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt I}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>\end{array}

If
s_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}s^{\prime}_{1}
, then:

\hskip-28.45274pt\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\tt I}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s_{1}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s_{1}\right>\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-11.0pt}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt I}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc s^{\prime}_{1}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{1}\right>\right>\end{array}

∎

Corollary 11
(Confluence of
\rightarrow_{{\tt I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
)

Let
t,u_{1},u_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{1}
and
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u_{2}
. Then there
is
s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that the diagram below commutes.

\begin{array}[]{ccc}t&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}^{*}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&u_{1}\\
{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}&&{}_{*}\hskip-4.0pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\hskip-7.0pt}\\
u_{2}&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}^{*}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&s\end{array}

Proof

Immediate consequence of the diamond for
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}

(
Lem.

41
).
∎

Lemma 42
(Internal Diligence)

Let
t,u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}}u
.
If
u
is a
\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!
-
NF
, then
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}{\tt ad}}u
.

Proof

Just apply
Lem.

14
to
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}}
,
since its hypotheses are fulfilled
(
Cors.

11
and
40
).
∎

0.C.2
Bang Factorization

Definition 12

Surface reduction

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}
is extended to contexts
as expected:

•

either the redex occurs in a
subterm
of some surface context,

•

or the redex occurs in a surface
subcontext
, where, in particular,

{\tt s\oc}
-redexes are only defined
as

t[x\backslash(\oc u)[x_{1}\backslash u_{1}]\ldots[x_{i}\backslash{\tt F}_{i}]\ldots[x_{n}\backslash u_{n}]]
, but not
as
t[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc{\tt F}\right>]
or
{\tt F}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc u\right>]
.

Remark that in particular
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}
reduction cannot make
the bang affecting the substituted subcontext disappear. Examples of
the first case are
\diamond[x\backslash(\lambda w.w)z]\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\diamond[x\backslash w[w\backslash z]]
,
\diamond\,\mathsf{der}(\oc z)\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\diamond\,z
and

\lambda x.\diamond z[z\backslash\oc x]\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\lambda x.\diamond x
,
while examples of the second case are
(\lambda x.x)\diamond\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}x[x\backslash\diamond]
and
\diamond(x[x\backslash\oc y])\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\diamond y
, but
(xx)[x\backslash\oc\diamond]
and

(\diamond x)[x\backslash\oc y]
are
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}
-irreducible.

Lemma 43

Let
t\in\Lambda_{\oc}
. Then,

1.

For every
{\tt I}\in\mathbb{I}
, if
{\tt I}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}{\tt F}
then
{\tt F}\in\mathbb{I}
.

2.

For every
{\tt S}\in\mathbb{S}
, if
{\tt S}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}{\tt F}
then
{\tt F}\in\mathbb{S}
.

3.

If
{\tt F}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}{\tt F}^{\prime}
, then

{\tt F}\!\left<t\right>\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}{\tt F}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>
.

4.

If
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}t^{\prime}
, then
{\tt S}\!\left<t\right>\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}{\tt S}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>
.

Proof

All the points are by straightforward induction on
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}
.
∎

See
7

Proof

We prove the following properties:

1.

(Termination)
The reductions

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}
,
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}
and
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
are
terminating.

2.

(Row-swaps)

(a)

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\ \subseteq\ \rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}
;

(b)

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\ \subseteq\ \rightarrow^{+}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}
;

(c)

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\ \subseteq\ \rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\,\cup\,\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
.

3.

(Diagonal-swaps)

(a)

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\ \subseteq\ \rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}
;

(b)

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\ \subseteq\ \rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}
;

(c)

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\ \subseteq\ \rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}
;

(d)

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\ \subseteq\ \rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\;\cup\;\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}
;

(e)

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\ \subseteq\ \rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
;

(f)

\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\ \subseteq\ \rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
.

thus deducing that
(\Lambda_{\oc},\;\{{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>\!,\,{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>\!,\,{\tt F}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\})
is an
SFS
.

1.

(Termination)
Reduction
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}
is terminating by
Lem.

9
.
Reduction
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}
is terminating by
Lem.

10
.
The proof that reduction
\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}
is terminating is an easy adaptation of the proof in
[
10
, Lemma 2.9]
to
dBANG
.

2.

(Row-swaps)
Let
t,u,s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
.

(a)

If
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}u\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}s
then
t={\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.t_{1}\right>t_{2}\right>
and
u={\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\right>
for some
{\tt I}\in\mathbb{I}
. Note that the step
u\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}s

can only occur inside
{\tt I}
so that
{\tt I}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}{\tt F}
and
{\tt F}={\tt I}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{I}
by

Lem.

43
.
1
. Thus

s={\tt I}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\right>
. Using

Lem.

43
.
3
:

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}t=&{\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.t_{1}\right>t_{2}\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&{\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\right>&=u\\[5.69046pt]
&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\\[5.69046pt]
&{\tt I}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.t_{1}\right>t_{2}\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&{\tt I}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\right>&=s\end{array}

(b)

If
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}u\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}s
then
t={\tt I}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc t_{2}\right>]\right>
and
u={\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}\{x\backslash t_{2}\}\right>\right>
for some
{\tt I}\in\mathbb{I}
.
Note that the step
u\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}s
can occur in two different ways:

•

either inside
{\tt I}
so that
{\tt I}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}{\tt I}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{I}
; thus

s={\tt I}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}\{x\backslash t_{2}\}\right>\right>
; using

Lem.

43
.
3
:

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}t=&{\tt I}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc t_{2}\right>]\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&{\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}\{x\backslash t_{2}\}\right>\right>&=u\\[5.69046pt]
&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\\[5.69046pt]
&{\tt I}^{\prime}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc t_{2}\right>]\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&{\tt I}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}\{x\backslash t_{2}\}\right>\right>&=s\end{array}

•

{\tt I}={\tt S}\!\left<r[y\backslash{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc{\tt S}^{\prime}\right>]\right>
with
{\tt S},{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\in\mathbb{S}
; so
u={\tt S}\!\left<r[y\backslash{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}\{x\backslash t_{2}\}\right>\right>\right>]\right>
and
s={\tt S}\!\left<{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<r\{y\backslash{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}\{x\backslash t_{2}\}\right>\right>\}\right>\right>
;
using

Lem.

43
.
3
:

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}t=&{\tt S}\!\left<r[y\backslash{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc t_{2}\right>]\right>\right>]\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{8.5359pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&{\tt S}\!\left<r[y\backslash{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}\{x\backslash t_{2}\}\right>\right>\right>]\right>&=u\\[5.69046pt]
&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{14.22636pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{14.22636pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\\[5.69046pt]
u^{\prime}=&{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<r\{y\backslash{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc t_{2}\right>]\right>\}\right>\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{8.5359pt}{0.0pt}}}^{*}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<r\{y\backslash{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}\{x\backslash t_{2}\}\right>\right>\}\right>\right>&=s\end{array}

We conclude by observing that the reduction sequence
u^{\prime}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{F}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}s
can be rearranged so that
u^{\prime}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}s
because all

{\tt s\oc}
-redexes
t_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc t_{2}\right>]

occurring in
r\{y\backslash{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc t_{2}\right>]\right>\}
are not overlapping
(as they replace different occurrences of
y
in
r
),
hence they can be fired independently, first the surface
ones and then the internal ones.

{\tt I}={\tt S}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}[y\backslash{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc s^{\prime}\right>]\right>
for
some
{\tt I}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{I}
and
s^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
: Thus

\begin{array}[]{rcl}t&=&{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<\oc t_{2}\right>]\right>[y\backslash{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc s^{\prime}\right>]\right>\\
u&=&{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\!\left<t_{1}\{x\backslash\oc t_{2}\}\right>\right>[y\backslash{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc s^{\prime}\right>]\right>\\
s&=&{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\{y\backslash s^{\prime}\}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\{y\backslash s^{\prime}\}\!\left<t_{1}\{y\backslash s^{\prime}\}\{x\backslash\oc t_{2}\{y\backslash s^{\prime}\}\}\right>\right>\right>\right>\end{array}

Then, by taking
u^{\prime}={\tt S}\!\left<{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\{y\backslash s^{\prime}\}\!\left<t_{1}\{y\backslash s^{\prime}\}[x\backslash{\tt L}\{y\backslash s^{\prime}\}\!\left<\oc t_{2}\{y\backslash s^{\prime}\}\right>]\right>\right>\right>
, one has:

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}t&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&u\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\\[5.69046pt]
u^{\prime}&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&s\end{array}

(c)

If
t\rightarrow_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}u\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}s
then
t={\tt I}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc t^{\prime}\right>)\right>
and
u={\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right>
for some
{\tt I}\!\in\!\mathbb{I}
.
Note that the step
u\rightarrow_{{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}s
can occur inside
{\tt I}
in two different ways:

•

either
{\tt I}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}{\tt I}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{I}
;
thus,
s={\tt I}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right>
;
using
Lem.

43
.
3
:

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}t=&{\tt I}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc t^{\prime}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right>&=u\\[5.69046pt]
&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\\[5.69046pt]
&{\tt I}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc t^{\prime}\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt I}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right>&=s\end{array}

•

or
{\tt I}={\tt S}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc{\tt S}^{\prime}\right>)\right>\rightarrow_{{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<{\tt S}^{\prime}\right>\right>\in\mathbb{S}
;
using
Lem.

43
.
3
:

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}t=&{\tt S}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc t^{\prime}\right>)\right>\right>)\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{8.5359pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt S}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right>\right>)\right>&=u\\[5.69046pt]
&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{14.22636pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{14.22636pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}\\[5.69046pt]
&{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathsf{der}({\tt L}\!\left<\oc t^{\prime}\right>)\right>\right>\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{8.5359pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right>\right>\right>&=s\end{array}

3.

(Diagonal-swaps)
Let
t,u,s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
.

(a)

Let
t\rightarrow_{{\tt I}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}u\rightarrow_{{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}s
then
t={\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.t_{1}\right>t_{2}\right>
and
u={\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\right>
for some
{\tt I}\in\mathbb{I}
.
Note that the step
u\rightarrow_{{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}s
can occur in two different ways:

•

either
{\tt I}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}{\tt I}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{I}
; thus,
s={\tt I}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\right>
; using

Lem.

43
.
3
:

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}t=&{\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.t_{1}\right>t_{2}\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&{\tt I}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\right>&=u\\[5.69046pt]
&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}&&\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\xleftarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}$}}_{\mathbb{S}\!\left<{\tt s\oc}\right>}\\[5.69046pt]
&{\tt I}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.t_{1}\right>t_{2}\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>}&{\tt I}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\right>&=s\end{array}

•

or
{\tt I}={\tt S}\!\left<r[y\backslash{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc{\tt S}^{\prime}\right>]\right>
with
{\tt S},{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\in\mathbb{S}
; so,
u={\tt S}\!\left<r[y\backslash{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\right>\right>]\right>
and

s={\tt S}\!\left<{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<r\{y\backslash{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\}\right>\right>
;
using
Lem.

43
.
3
:

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}t=&{\tt S}\!\left<r[y\backslash{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<\lambda x.t_{1}\right>t_{2}\right>\right>]\right>&\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\rule{8.5359pt}{0.0pt}}}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&{\tt S}\!\left<r[y\backslash{\tt L}^{\prime}\!\left<\oc{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt L}\!\left<t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\right>\right>]\right>&=u\\[5.69046pt]

0.C.3
Call-by-Name Factorization

Lemma 44

The following two properties hold:

1.

(
dCBN

\rightarrow

dBANG
)

Let
{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
, then

{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\in\mathbb{I}
.

2.

(
dBANG

\rightarrow

dCBN
)
Let
{\tt I}\in\mathbb{I}
and
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}={\tt I}
, then
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
.

Proof

1.

(
dCBN

\rightarrow

dBANG
)
By induction on

{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
:

{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=s\,{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
for some
s\in\Lambda
and
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
, then

{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}=(s\,{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}})^{\tt n}=s^{\tt n}\,\oc{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}=(s^{\tt n}\,\diamond)\!\left<\oc{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\right>\in\mathbb{S}\!\left<\oc\mathbb{F}\right>\subseteq\mathbb{I}
.

{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=s[x\backslash{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}]
for some
s\in\Lambda
and
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
, then

{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}=(s[x\backslash{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}])^{\tt n}=s^{\tt n}[x\backslash\oc{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}]=(s^{\tt n}[x\backslash\diamond])\!\left<\oc{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}\right>\in\mathbb{S}\!\left<\oc\mathbb{F}\right>\subseteq\mathbb{I}
.

{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}={\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\!\left<{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>
for some

{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\backslash\{\diamond\}
and

{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
: By induction on
{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
:

{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=\diamond
: Then by
i.h.
on

{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}
, one has that
{{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}\in\mathbb{I}
which concludes this case since

{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}={\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\!\left<{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>={\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}
.

{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=\lambda x.{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}
: Then by
i.h.
on
{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}
, one has that

({\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>)^{\tt n}\in\mathbb{I}
and thus
{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}=(\lambda x.{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>)^{\tt n}=\lambda x.({\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>)^{\tt n}=(\lambda x.\diamond)\!\left<({\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>)^{\tt n}\right>\in\mathbb{S}\!\left<\mathbb{I}\right>\subseteq\mathbb{I}
.

{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}={\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\,t
: Then by

i.h.
on
{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}
, one has that

({\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>)^{\tt n}\in\mathbb{I}
and thus
{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}=({\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>t)^{\tt n}=({\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>)^{\tt n}\,\oc t^{\tt n}=(\diamond\,\oc t^{\tt n})\!\left<({\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>)^{\tt n}\right>\in\mathbb{S}\!\left<\mathbb{I}\right>\subseteq\mathbb{I}
.

{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}={\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}[x\backslash t]
: Then by
i.h.
on
{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}
, one has that

({\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>)^{\tt n}\in\mathbb{I}
and thus
{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}=({\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>[x\backslash t])^{\tt n}=({\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>)^{\tt n}[x\backslash\oc t^{\tt n}]=(\diamond[x\backslash\oc t^{\tt n}])\!\left<({\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>)^{\tt n}\right>\in\mathbb{S}\!\left<\mathbb{I}\right>\subseteq\mathbb{I}
.

2.

(
dBANG

\rightarrow

dCBN
)
Let
{\tt I}\in\mathbb{I}
and
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}={\tt I}
. By induction on

{\tt I}\in\mathbb{I}
:

{\tt I}=\oc{\tt F}
for some
{\tt F}\in\mathbb{F}
. Impossible since one cannot have

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}=\oc{\tt F}
by definition of
the
dCBN
embedding on contexts.

{\tt I}={\tt S}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>
for some
{\tt S}\in\mathbb{S}\backslash\{\diamond\}
: By induction on

{\tt S}\in\mathbb{S}
:

{\tt S}=\diamond
: Then

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}={\tt S}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>={\tt I}^{\prime}
and by
i.h.
on
{\tt I}^{\prime}
, one concludes
that
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
.

{\tt S}=\lambda x.{\tt S}^{\prime}
: Then

{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>=\lambda x.{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>
thus necessarily

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=\lambda x.{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}
for some
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that
{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>
. By
i.h.
on
{\tt S}^{\prime}
, one
has that
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
so that
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=\lambda x.{\tt F}^{\prime}=(\lambda x.\diamond)\!\left<{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>\in\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\!\left<\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\right>\subseteq\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
.

{\tt S}={\tt S}^{\prime}s^{\prime}
: Then

{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>={\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>s^{\prime}
thus necessarily

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}={\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\,s
for some
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
and
s\in\Lambda
such that

{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>

and
\oc s^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}
. By
i.h.
on
{\tt S}^{\prime}
,
one has that
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
so that

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}={\tt F}^{\prime}\,s=(\diamond\,s)\!\left<{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>\in\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\!\left<\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\right>\subseteq\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
.

{\tt S}=s^{\prime}\,{\tt S}^{\prime}
: Then

{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>=s^{\prime}\,{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>
thus necessarily

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=s\,{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}
for some
s\in\Lambda
and
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that

s^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}
and

\oc{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>
, which concludes this case since

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=s\,{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in s\,\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\subseteq\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
.

{\tt S}={\tt S}^{\prime}[x\backslash s^{\prime}]
: Then

{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>={\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>[x\backslash s^{\prime}]
thus necessarily

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}={\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}[x\backslash s]
for some
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
and
s\in\Lambda
such that

{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>

and
\oc s^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}
. By
i.h.
on
{\tt S}^{\prime}
,
one has that
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
so that

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}={\tt F}^{\prime}[x\backslash s]=(\diamond[x\backslash s])\!\left<{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\right>\in\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\!\left<\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}\right>\subseteq\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
.

{\tt S}=s^{\prime}[x\backslash{\tt S}^{\prime}]
: Then

{\tt S}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>=s^{\prime}[x\backslash{\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>]
thus necessarily

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=s[x\backslash{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}]
for some
s\in\Lambda
and
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
such that

s^{\tt n}=s^{\prime}
and

\oc{{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}}^{\tt n}={\tt S}^{\prime}\!\left<{\tt I}^{\prime}\right>
, which concludes this case since

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}=s[x\backslash{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\prime}]\in s[x\backslash\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}]\subseteq\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
.

{\tt S}=\mathsf{der}({\tt S}^{\prime})
: Impossible
since one cannot have
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}^{\tt n}=\mathsf{der}({\tt S})
by definition of the
dCBN
embedding
on contexts.
∎

8

Proof

Immediate consequence of
Lems.

and

.

0.C.4
Call-by-Value Factorization

Lemma 45

The following property hold:

1.

Let
{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}
, then
{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v},{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v}\!\left<\mathsf{der}(\diamond)\right>,{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v*},{\tt rmDst}({\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}})^{\tt v*}\in\mathbb{I}
.

2.

Let
{\tt I}\in\mathbb{I}
and
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}
such that either
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v}={\tt I}
or
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v*}={\tt I}
or

{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v}\!\left<\mathsf{der}(\diamond)\right>={\tt I}
, then
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}
.

Proof

1.

Notice that if
{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}
then

{\tt rmDst}({\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}})\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}
, and that if

{\tt I}\in\mathbb{I}
then
{\tt I}\!\left<\mathsf{der}(\diamond)\right>\in\mathbb{I}
. Thanks to these observations, it becomes
sufficient to show that if
{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}
, then

{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v}\in{\tt I}
and

{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v*}\in{\tt I}
.

Let
{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}\in\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt N}}}
, then by construction

{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}={\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}\!\left<\lambda x.{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}\right>
for some

{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}\in\mathbb{S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}
and
{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}
.
We distinguish two cases:

{\tt func}({\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}})
: Using
Lems.

26
and
36
:

\begin{array}[]{r cl cl}{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v}&=&{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v*}\!\left<{\tt strip}((\lambda x.{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}})^{\tt v})\right>\\
&=&{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v*}\!\left<{\tt strip}(\oc\lambda x.\oc{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v})\right>\\
&=&{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v*}\!\left<\lambda x.\oc{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v}\right>&\in&\mathbb{S}\!\left<\lambda x.\oc\mathbb{F}\right>\;\subseteq\;\mathbb{S}\!\left<\oc\mathbb{F}\right>\;\subseteq\;\mathbb{I}\end{array}

\neg{\tt func}({\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}})
: Using
Lems.

26
and
36
:

\begin{array}[]{r cl cl}{\tt I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v}&=&{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v}\!\left<(\lambda x.{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}})^{\tt v}\right>\\
&=&{\tt S}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v}\!\left<\oc\lambda x.\oc{\tt F}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}^{\tt v}\right>&\in&\mathbb{S}\!\left<\oc\lambda x.\oc\mathbb{F}\right>\;\subseteq\;\mathbb{S}\!\left<\oc\mathbb{F}\right>\;\subseteq\;\mathbb{I}\end{array}

2.

By straightforward induction on
{\tt I}\in\mathbb{I}
.
∎

Corollary 12

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
and
u^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that

{\tt NF}_{\mathbb{I}\!\left<{\tt d\oc}\right>\!}(u^{\prime})
. Then,

•

(Stability)
:
t^{\tt v}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}}u^{\prime}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\exists\,u\in\Lambda,\;u^{\tt v}=u^{\prime}

•

(Normal Forms)
:
t\text{ is a $\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}$-{\tt NF} }\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad t^{\tt v}\text{ is a $\mathbb{I}$-{\tt NF} }

•

(Simulations)
:
t\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}_{\scalebox{0.55}{{\tt V}}}}u\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad t^{\tt v}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbb{I}}u^{\tt v}

Moreover, the number of
{\tt dB}/{\tt sV}
-steps
on the left matches the number

{\tt dB}/{\tt s\oc}
-steps on the right.

Proof

Using
Lem.

34
with

Lems.

45
,
42
and
38
.
∎
